Columbia coach Megan Griffith returns from suspension for big game vs. Michigan

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Without head coach Megan Griffith on the sidelines for its first three games of the season, the Columbia women's basketball team didn't just survive — it thrived.

The Lions went 3-0 against Davidson, Rhode Island and Belmont, a trio of squads that ranked in the top 110 of NET last year, a metric the NCAA uses to sort teams. Columbia won those games by an average margin of 13.3 points with associate coach Tyler Cordell making the in-game decisions and adjustments.

"Yeah, she should just take my job," Griffith said with a laugh during a Zoom call with reporters. "What I loved about it was how she just jumped right into it. I think Ty really understood the game planning aspect from my standpoint and how I think about it, and she put her spin on it. Which for her, that is so easy because she built the program with me."

Griffith is now back on the sidelines for Columbia following a three-game suspension, returning to the lead chair on the bench for perhaps the toughest stretch of the Lions' schedule. On Thursday, Nov. 12, they'll host No. 2 Michigan at Levien Gymnasium in New York City, travel to No. 18 Maryland on Sunday, Nov. 15, then host No. 5 Duke on Saturday, Nov. 21.

Columbia announced it was suspending Griffith — an alum of the Ivy League institution who is in her 10th season leading the Lions — in October following a review of the program by an outside firm after the university received a complaint about her behavior during practices from past seasons.

In a statement, Columbia said the review did not require notice to the NCAA of any rules violations and did not involve any allegations of violations of the university's anti-discrimination policies, Title IX or related policies. According to the statement, "Griffith expressed regret for the times in which her interactions with the team did not align with the university's expectations."

USA TODAY Sports was provided with a statement from current Columbia players, too. They said they "fully stand with and support" Griffith and that the complaint was "not indicative of our experience as part of this program."

During a call with reporters on Tuesday, Nov. 10, Griffith didn't discuss the reasoning behind her suspension, but talked about the positives that she took away from it.

"What a unique experience this has been. The biggest benefit was, I think, bringing my staff into how I game plan and think about the game, bringing our players more deeply into it, and then I got to watch a lot and coach the details of the game in a very different way," Griffith said. "I got to observe from a very different vantage point of a game. And obviously, that's not what I wanted. You want to be with your team, right?

"But it was a great opportunity to learn in a different way, and I think our team and my staff did a phenomenal job of stepping up and battling together."

Griffith, 41, played at Columbia from 2003-2007 and was a two-time All-Ivy League selection. After playing professionally in Europe, she joined the coaching ranks by becoming an assistant for Courtney Banghart at Princeton during a stretch where the Tigers made three NCAA Tournaments.

In 2016, Griffith took the reins at her alma mater and has turned Columbia into a consistent mid-major power. In the last five seasons, the Lions were 125-35 with three regular season Ivy League titles, two at-large NCAA Tournament appearances, a runner-up finish in the WNIT in 2023 and a WBIT championship last season. Columbia also won an NCAA Tournament game in 2025, defeating Washington in the First Four.

The March Madness bids and victory — and the WBIT title — were the first in Columbia's history. The program that Griffith has turned the Lions into is one that Power 4 teams and national title contenders are willing to play in the non-conference slate now, because they know Columbia will provide a competitive game and a sufficient measuring stick.

And for Columbia, pulling off an upset in one of these games can be the bullet point on the resume it needs to secure a berth in March Madness.

"It's a gauntlet. I love a challenge," Griffith said. "We have to prove a resume for March. Not that March hinges on (these games), but it gives you another chance, right? So, very intentionally, we always look for a couple of these Quad 1 games.

"Teams like Michigan, teams like Duke, they're not afraid to play anywhere. Those are teams trying to prepare themselves for hostile environments and playing against strong teams. I think that's just a good sign of a program that's really trying to test themselves for March and make a deep run."

Led by reigning Ivy League Player of the Year Riley Weiss, Columbia also brought back key contributors in Mia Broom, Fliss Henderson and 6-foot-3 German forward Hilke Feldrappe. Through the transfer portal, former Big Ten Rookie of the Year Mary Ashley Stevenson has also joined Columbia this season after spending one season apiece at Purdue and Stanford.

"They can turn it on when they want. I think we need to realize they can turn it on more. I think we're sometimes assessing the game and I'm like, 'Impose yourself. Be tone-setters,'" Griffith said. "I just want to see what we're made of a little bit more, right? Let's really test ourselves. We're throwing ourselves fully into the fire here."

To have a chance against a pair of teams in Michigan and Duke that made the Elite Eight last season and a consistent winner in Brenda Frese's Maryland, Columbia will need to push itself to play at its very best.

Kirk Cousins has 3-word message for Fernando Mendoza after Raiders trade

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Fernandomania has officially begun in the desert.

With the Las Vegas Raiders trading quarterback Kirk Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings in before Tuesday's NFL trade deadline, Las Vegas are expected to turn to Fernando Mendoza, the No. 1 pick in the draft earlier this year, under center.

Mendoza is slated to make his first career start against the defending Super Bowl champion Seattle Seahawks at home on Sunday, Nov. 15 (4:05 ET).

Mendoza's lone regular-season action came during his debut, at the end of a Week 4 road loss to the New England Patriots on Oct. 11. He finished 6-for-8 passing for 48 yards.

Mendoza's first two opponents boast vaunted defensive units. Seattle's defense, dubbed the "Dark Side" for a reason, did not allow a touchdown last week to the undermanned Arizona Cardinals. Looking ahead, the Raiders face the Denver Broncos on the road in Week 11. Denver ranks in the top 10 in points allowed per game (20.7), sacks (26) and takeaways (12).

"I feel that Fernando has done a great job," Cousins told reporters Oct. 26. "He has a bright future ahead. I'm going to try and go out there and play as well as I possibly can and try and win and give him a good example to learn from."

On his Instagram story, Cousins offered a goodbye to "Raider Nation" and a message to Mendoza, whom Cousins tagged in the post.

Toughening matters for Mendoza on the field will be the lack of a rushing attack for the Raiders. First-round talent Ashton Jeanty from the 2025 draft has not worked out, although rookie Mike Washington has shown some juice in the backfield. Still, Las Vegas ranks 31st in rushing yards per game (71.3) and is last in yards per rush (2.9).

Similar to Cousins, Mendoza will likely have to shoulder much of the output in the Raiders' offense.

"The game has to be balanced," head coach Klint Kubiak said following the team's most recent loss to the San Francisco 49ers that dropped their record to 4-5. "You can't be a running and throwing team when you're throwing it every time. That's not easy.

"You got to start the game a lot faster. You got to go toe-to-toe in the first half so that the second half isn't a throwing fest."

The good news is that tight end Brock Bowers ranks ninth in the league in receiving yards and provides a crucial red-zone target with six touchdowns already this season. Wide receiver Tre Tucker emerged as a reliable downfield option for Cousins, which gives Mendoza more relief in the passing game.

Brokies, This Met Gala Is for You

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

T-shirts revolutionize the Costume Institute’s programming, the sociability of MoMA’s Brancusi exhibit, and the curators of the 2028 Whitney Biennial announced.

A saturated blue sky and cascades of complementary golden-yellow foliage laid the foundation for a vibrant, picturesque Diwali weekend in the suburbs. It wasn’t the most festive Diwali for me — instead of lighting diyas and playing with sparklers, I spent a majority of the weekend hand-feeding a friendly yard squirrel while wearing some bullshit pajamas extracted from the depths of my childhood closet.

Think I’m slumming it? Well, think again! Because those dilapidated pajamas are almost certainly what inspired the theme for next year’s Met Gala and related Costume Institute exhibit: extraOrdinary: Fashion and the Poetics of the Everyday, a program “devoted to the undersung beauty and allure of quotidian garments.” (That’s code for jeans.)

(And t-shirts. And raincoats. Maybe pajamas, but also maybe not.)

It’s an exasperated, last-minute pivot after controversial British fashion designer John Galliano, who was central to the Costume Institute’s original program for 2027, withdrew from participating entirely this August in response to the public backlash toward his racist behavior in the past. Between “elevating” daily wear into runway fashion and exploring how “humble, even ‘disposable’ materials” like paper and aluminum can become wearable, this enormous exercise in putting a positive spin on gentrifying economic recession is at odds with NYC residents who are buckling from profound unaffordability as the city speedruns its transformation into a private equity playground.

I guess I can’t really say which is worse — the current standard of celebrities in bespoke, extravagant garments from elite fashion houses hanging off their GLP-1 frames, or the notion that a Met Gala dress code calling for aluminum foil pants, a shrunken tee, and maybe a jacket made from hole-punched plastic circles is what’ll push people to consider everyday wear in a different light.

However, before we get too pressed, we have to acknowledge that this is a big moment for the Bad Bunny x Zara collection! On the plus side, it might make it easier for some ~lowly civs~ to sneak in and eat all the cheese cubes. Dress for the job you want, right?

Speaking of jobs — Whitney Museum curator Meg Onli returns as a co-curator for the 2028 Whitney Biennial, sharing the stage with director and dancer Zack Winokur, who co-founded the interdisciplinary American Modern Opera Company in 2017. I found the 2026 Biennial to be a textbook case of the whole being less than the sum of its parts. Let’s see how Winokur’s hand in artistic performance direction manifests in this curatorial behemoth; I’m sure he’s used to working with a lot of moving parts.

Back to the here and now, I’m long overdue for multiple museum visits at the peak of the season. The days keep slipping through my fingers before I’ve had a chance to visit the Krasner Pollock show at The Met or the Architects of Liberation at the MoMA. Mayor Mamdani actually paid a visit to the MoMA show before I did, and he’s significantly busier than I am, so there’s really no excuse!

In another nudge for me to make the journey uptown, MoMA recently opened its Brancusi exhibition. With a replica of Brancusi’s studio and a selection of rare media documenting his work environment and the social atmosphere he cultivated between his sculptures and his guests alike, the exhibition spotlights the artist’s labor-intensive process as an art form in itself.

Critic Aruna D’Souza noted that the “sculptures seem to be maintaining COVID-era social distancing protocols,” and chalked that awkward dynamic up to the MoMA’s renovated gallery layout, recalling the previously approachable setup’s capacity to “allow for art objects to talk to each other.”

On Sunday, I bore witness to a conversation that made me think about Brancusi’s glinting buffed bronzes and smooth marbles. The chatter between a yellowing tree branch, an overturned recycling bin, and the white picket fence behind them might have been too maximalist compared to the Romanian sculptor’s sleek, pared-down modernism. Nevertheless, I pulled over to eavesdrop.

Among the historic wave of triumphs for Democrats in yesterday’s midterms, New York State Assemblywoman Claire Valdez, an artist, former cultural worker, and union organizer, was elected to represent New York’s 7th Congressional District in the United States House of Representatives. | Anna Lee

Having co-curated the 2024 Biennial, Onli returns to the next edition with Winokur, who founded the American Modern Opera Company. | Anna Lee

The artist’s work is glorious and resonant, but the Museum of Modern Art has a space problem, or an elegance problem, or a crowd mitigation problem. | Aruna D’Souza

A little more legibility would’ve benefited Alison Nguyen’s fragmented short film about the famed NYC artists’ housing complex. | Rhea Nayyar

This residency at the U-M Stamps School, in partnership with the U-M Museum of Art (UMMA), offers artists a $20,000 honorarium, housing, studio space, and a culminating exhibition in UMMA’s prominent Stenn Gallery.

The Bayeux tapestry, a new biography of Edward Hopper, The Met's exhibition catalogue on Krasner and Pollock, what architecture is, and more

Funding for tuition, healthcare, and stipends is available. Apply to the University of Maryland, Baltimore County program by Feb 1 to be considered for financial support.

Generously funded, renowned faculty and visiting artists, spacious studios, and an exhibition at the Manetti Shrem Museum of Art.

Ummm. . . Do you know how Medicare works and what options she has (or will have)?

Drug plan is extra cost and depends on what meds you take. If you want dental insurance, that is outside of Medicare and we each pay about $50 a month for that. . .

I don't mean to hijack this thread but you may need to reconsider your plan.

Dental insurance is of limited value and a few root canals and implants can be many thousands of dollars.

Ring cameras go mobile as Amazon debuts AI-powered security drone and robot

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amazon’s roaming Ring Robotics devices will debut in late 2026 and early 2027.

Amazon is betting that many Ring customers will also want an AI-powered security drone and robot patrolling their homes or offices. But the company’s upcoming Ring Robotic products are also certain to intensify privacy concerns previously raised around a prior Amazon attempt to popularize a flying security camera.

The Ring Rover Cam and Ring Drone Cam are expected to reach customers starting at the end of 2026 and in early 2027, respectively, according to an Amazon announcement posted on November 10. Amazon describes the devices as relying on an AI Pilot system that uses each device’s cameras along with “specialized vision models, on-device sensors and cloud processing” to plan patrol routes while autonomously navigating around obstacles such as furniture.

Both devices also use that AI Pilot system to digitally map the floor plan of the customer’s home or business. This setup process occurs after the customer carries the Ring drone through their indoor spaces or allows the Ring Rover to do some autonomous exploration.

The AI Pilot can supposedly use that guided- or self-tour information to identify specific rooms and suggest patrol points near side doors, windows, or even sensitive locations such as a cash register in the case of a business.

However, flying and roving cameras with AI-enabled features could introduce new privacy risks for homes and businesses, on top of existing concerns involving fixed Ring cameras installed in room corners or as doorbell cameras.

Amazon’s Ring Drone appears to be a direct successor to the company’s Ring Always Home Cam that was only temporarily available for purchase by invitation as a “Day 1 Edition” product in September 2021—even having the same starting price of $250.

But the Ring Drone still requires authorization from the Federal Communications Commission before going on sale. Assuming that comes through, Amazon aims to make the drone available on January 27, 2027, with customers able to register for email updates through the product page.

Onboard camera capability includes a 148-degree field of view and 360-degree rotation with a 2K video resolution. Two LED lights also activate in low-light conditions to help illuminate the scene for the drone video camera.

However, the drone is limited to just 8 minutes of flight time when operating on a “new, fully-charged battery under controlled conditions” according to the product description. It comes with a docking station for charging.

Another caveat in the product description is that the Ring Drone Cam may experience degraded performance when navigating in environments with reflective surfaces such as “polished marble or wet tile,” blank matte walls without any features, or transparent surfaces like frameless glass and mirrors.

Still, Amazon touts the drone as providing an “autonomous way to check on your home that has never existed before,” whether the customer is away or at home. The Ring Drone can be controlled directly from the Alexa Home Map interactive map that shows all of a customer’s Amazon-linked devices.

Meanwhile, Amazon has pitched the Ring Rover Cam priced at $1,000 as “an autonomous security robot designed for business.” This product is already available for preorder for those so inclined and scheduled to start shipping to customers on December 16, 2026.

The company described the Ring Rover as being inspired by customer feedback involving another invite-only beta product, the Amazon Astro robot designed for home monitoring.

The Ring Rover can livestream and record in up to 4K video resolution when patrolling or while doing a 360-degree scan at a designated location. Videos triggered by doorbell rings, motion events and “Live View” events are stored in the cloud for sharing and replaying for up to 180 days.

Like the Ring Drone, the Ring Rover can automatically respond to alerts from connected Ring devices by following its preprogrammed patrol points. But the wheeled robot is only designed to operate within a single-floor, indoor environment because it lacks stair-climbing capability.

Amazon’s product description also warns that the Rover is not compatible with “curved edges, sunken areas, flooring transitions greater than 1.375 inches (3.5 centimeters), and black glossy floors.”

To help compensate for its ground-based view, the Ring Rover comes with an automatically adjustable mast to peer over counters, shelving and other obstacles. It can supposedly cover up to 5,000 square feet (465 square meters), according to Amazon’s product description.

The Ring Rover also comes with additional security-related features such as a siren and light tower to loudly announce its presence, along with a microphone for two-way audio communication if the customer wants to address someone near the robot.

Amazon touts the new Ring Robotics products as offering “privacy and security without compromise,” with features such as LED indicators and screen notifications informing customers when video and audio recordings are active. But Ring, as an Amazon subsidiary, has been previously implicated in multiple privacy and security scandals.

Ring was previously charged in a 2023 Federal Trade Commission investigation with allowing employees and contractors to watch Ring video recordings of customers’ private spaces. The FTC also described Ring as having lackluster safeguards that allowed hackers to access 55,000 US customers’ accounts.

Videos taken by the new Ring Drone and Ring Rover will be protected with Ring’s Throw Away the Key Encryption that was introduced in September 2026, according to Amazon’s announcement. The digital rights nonprofit Electronic Frontier Foundation (EFF) has described this as an improvement over Ring’s former default settings, but still well short of end-to-end encryption.

The EFF has also called out what it describes as Ring founder Jamie Siminoff’s “surveillance-first-privacy-last” approach to Ring devices, including Ring reintroducing features that allow law enforcement to request footage or live-stream access from Ring users’ home security devices.

Amazon’s previous attempt to launch its Ring Always Home Cam drone also led to expanded privacy concerns. Ryan Calo, a law and technology researcher at the University of Washington, previously described such a flying security camera as providing new ways to surveil others indoors, along with giving companies much greater access to potentially sensitive or identifying details of homes and businesses.

There is currently no mention of the new Ring Robotics products implementing facial recognition or similar AI-powered technologies for identifying people. But starting in December 2025, Ring has allowed owners of fixed Ring doorbell cameras to turn on a “Familiar Faces” feature that uses facial recognition technology on visitors—and that is already the subject of a class action lawsuit.

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Inside OM Digital Solutions’ Okaya Manufacturing Site

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Inside OM Digital Solutions’ Okaya Manufacturing Site

I know how to take pictures, but ask me how the lens on my camera is made and I have a lot less to say. A visit to the Okaya Manufacturing Site in Japan, followed by some research afterward, gave me a lesson in a subject I’d never understood particularly well. I wanted to understand how the glass inside a lens is crafted, how the tools used in that process are prepared, and how people learn to do that work.

The facility was OM Digital Solutions’ Okaya Manufacturing Site in Nagano Prefecture, Japan. This was an overwhelming experience from a learning standpoint, so there was quite a bit that went over my head as we moved from station to station. Luckily, follow-up discussions with OM SYSTEM and research helped me fill in some of the gaps.

Okaya is the first optical lens manufacturing site in Japan for OM Digital Solutions, the company behind OM SYSTEM. It opened in 2025 and does two jobs. It produces lenses that are made in small quantities but come in many different types, and it works as a pilot factory, a place where the manufacturing processes for technically demanding new lenses are developed and proven before they’re used at scale.

The site works closely with the optical design team at the company’s headquarters in Hachioji, Tokyo. Designing a lens and figuring out how to build it are two different jobs that call for different expertise. Okaya’s engineers handle the second one, turning a design into a production process that actually works.

Mass production happens at the company’s factory in Vietnam. Before a production line for a new lens launches there, Okaya verifies the production technology, the manufacturing processes, and the work methods it will use. Working those out in Japan first is meant to make production in Vietnam more efficient and keep the quality consistent.

The place to begin is with an individual lens element, one of the many pieces of glass inside a camera lens. OM SYSTEM’s M.Zuiko Digital ED 40-150mm F2.8 PRO, for example, contains 16 of these elements. Before the lens can be assembled, each of these pieces of glass needs the correct curve and a smooth surface.

A piece of glass can be clear and look correct but still bend light the wrong way. A lens bends light to bring an image into focus on the camera’s sensor. Light from each point in the scene needs to come together at a matching point on the sensor. The curves of the glass help direct it there. If a curve is wrong, that light can spread around its intended point instead, making a small detail look blurry.

The sequence OM SYSTEM showed us at the Okaya Manufacturing Site starts with grinding, which gradually wears down the glass to form the basic curve. Finer grinding makes smaller adjustments to the glass. Polishing then wears away more material to give the surface the smoothness and accuracy it needs.

I got a small taste of this myself. During a workshop at Okaya, I tried lens polishing, including mounting the lens and taking it off again afterward. It looked simple. Then a slight mistake in my setup chipped the lens. That was enough to show me how delicate the process is and how much care and precision it takes.

Glass can look clear and smooth to the naked eye and still have flaws that affect the photograph. Tiny scratches, bumps, and dips, or a surface that strays slightly from its intended shape, can scatter light in directions it shouldn’t go. That can cause flare, the haze or bright spots stray light leaves in a picture, and lower the contrast, so the image looks flatter.

If the surface doesn’t match its designed shape, light won’t bend and come together the way the designers intended. So polishing has two jobs: leave the surface free of scratches, and reproduce the designed shape to within nanometers, or billionths of a meter. The engineers check the glass with a range of measuring instruments, but they also rely on experience and skill to make delicate adjustments to each lens.

Then they have to do it again on the next piece of glass, and the one after that. Even a slight difference between individual lenses can change how light travels through the finished lens and affect image quality.

Each glass element also needs to sit in the right position inside the lens. Imagine fitting one into a holder. It could fit neatly while the curved faces that bend light sit slightly off-center. That mismatch can send light to the wrong place. A manufacturing step called centering corrects that mismatch before assembly.

Getting the curve right and getting the glass into the right position both matter to the photograph. Even when we focus correctly, errors in the glass’s shape or alignment can leave fine details looking soft.

Even the tools OM Digital Solutions uses at the Okaya Manufacturing Site need preparation before they can work on the glass. These are the parts fitted to the machines that grind and polish the lens elements. The tools the engineers use to work on the glass need smooth, accurately made surfaces, too.

One of these tools has a small, round face that touches the glass. To prepare it, an engineer uses a second tool: a disc on the end of a handle. The engineer holds the disc against the first tool’s face, polishing the two surfaces together.

The accuracy of the tool affects the accuracy of the surface it produces on the glass.

I was surprised to see that they still prepare some of their tools by hand. The precision required helped me appreciate the care the engineers bring to preparing those tools and building the lenses. At Okaya, I saw veteran engineers working alongside younger colleagues to pass on their skills. Both generations were full of excitement and pride as they showed us how they make lenses in their facility.

That exchange between generations is a big part of how the skills developed at the Okaya Manufacturing Site get passed down. OM Digital Solutions says the younger engineers’ questions also encourage the veterans to keep improving their own skills. While watching them work, I could see how explaining a familiar task to someone else can make you think more carefully about the way you do it.

The training follows an apprenticeship model, with coaching taking place during manufacturing work such as polishing. Part of the challenge is passing on knowledge that comes from experience and can be difficult to put into written instructions.

With its younger engineers in mind, the company is also exploring other ways to pass on the skills and judgment that are hard to capture in a written document.

Along with their manufacturing skills, OM Digital Solutions also wants the experienced engineers to pass on their way of thinking about the work.

If I were explaining one of my photographs to another photographer, I’d want to talk about why I chose a particular aperture for depth of field or focused on a certain part of the scene. Understanding those choices could help them make decisions about their own photographs. Knowing why a manufacturing step is done a certain way could help younger engineers decide what to keep and where to make improvements. They can build on that experience to improve both how lenses are made and how those lenses perform.

When a photographer buys a lens, they expect it to meet the same standard as other copies of that model. That depends on being able to make its components accurately, again and again.

At Okaya, engineers test early versions of lens components for new products. They also check new manufacturing methods to see whether they are suitable for producing lenses in larger quantities. Once those methods have been checked, they can be transferred to the factory in Vietnam.

Some of this involves tasks as routine as loading parts into machines and taking them out again. They are automating repetitive jobs like these to help improve production quality and efficiency.

The relationship between the two sites continues long after production begins. If an issue turns up while a mass-production process is being verified at the Vietnam factory, the team at Okaya can reproduce the same issue on purpose. Recreating it gives the engineers a controlled way to analyze what went wrong, find the root cause, and develop a fix. Each round of that back-and-forth turns highly specialized know-how into a process the factory can repeat with the same quality, lens after lens.

Learning about OM Digital Solutions’ Okaya Manufacturing Site helped me understand the work, passion, and dedication that go into their lens making. I had never given the process much thought and, I admit, I assumed it was easier than it actually is. The hand preparation of the tools was a good example. Once I understood how a tool’s accuracy affects the glass it works on, I could see why that preparation needed so much care.

It also showed me how seriously the company takes quality. Nearly every step I learned about, from measuring a polished surface to deliberately recreating a problem from the Vietnam factory, exists to make sure a lens performs the way it was designed to, and that the next copy does too.

Understanding the work gave me more respect for the engineers who showed it to us and for the experience they are passing on to younger colleagues. As a travel photographer, I’ve often taken my gear for granted. I now have a better idea of what goes into making a lens and a much greater appreciation for the lenses that have helped me document the world over the last 15 years.

NFL trade deadline winners and losers: Kirk Cousins heads home, Bengals seemingly fail to understand the rules

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The 2026 NFL trade deadline has come and gone, and while we did see a couple of blockbuster trades in recent weeks, such as the Chicago Bears trading for Jeffery Simmons and the Jacksonville Jaguars swinging deals for three players in one day, the final 24 hours of the trade deadline were relatively quiet.

On Tuesday, the Minnesota Vikings brought back Kirk Cousins to replace Kyler Murray, and the Kansas City Chiefs added a new wide receiver in Josh Downs. However, there were plenty of other teams perceived to be "buyers" who stayed put, while other "sellers" were hesitant to part ways with their assets. In all, 23 teams combined to make 18 trades with 19 players and 34 picks changing hands. It was the Indianapolis Colts who led the way with an NFL-high four trades for six draft selections, although star running back Jonathan Taylor was not moved.

So, who won and who lost at the trade deadline? Let's take a look.

The Vikings and Las Vegas Raiders made the deal of the day, as new general manager Nolan Teasley sent a third-round pick to "Sin City" for Kirk Cousins. It feels like everyone is a winner in this situation.

The Vikings are a winner because they absolutely had to do something at the quarterback position. Kyler Murray wasn't cutting it, and then Carson Wentz registered one of the worst performances by a Vikings quarterback ever on Monday night with an 8.9 passer rating and a 32% completion rate. The Vikings were wasting the efforts of the best defense in the NFL and found themselves in a spot where they were forced to make an addition. Bringing back a familiar face who has been performing well this season with 20 touchdowns and eight interceptions is a good move.

Cousins actually has the most passing touchdowns (20) and career passing touchdowns (318) by a quarterback changing teams midseason, all-time. During his six seasons in Minnesota (2018-2023), he ranked top five in completion percentage, passing yards and passing touchdowns while going 50-37-1 as the starter.

The Raiders are a winner because they got a third-round pick for their rental quarterback in a season where they likely aren't making the playoffs. The Vikings didn't enter this year expecting to trade for a quarterback in November, which allowed the Raiders to drive up the price.

Kirk Cousins is a winner because he upgrades his own situation. He goes from 4-5 to 6-2, and has the opportunity to spark a legitimate Super Bowl run. That may sound like an exaggeration, but with Justin Jefferson, Jordan Addison and T.J. Hockenson, the Vikings have everything in place to make a run. All they needed was better quarterback play. It could be a storybook ending for Cousins.

Fernando Mendoza is a winner because he's the new starter in Vegas, although the No. 1 overall pick has to begin his NFL career against some aggressive defenses with the Seattle Seahawks on Sunday and then the Denver Broncos the following week.

Another winner is Justin Jefferson, because he gets his quarterback back. JJ began his NFL career with three straight 1,400-yard seasons — including a 1,809-yard campaign in 2022. Jefferson barely crossed 1,000 yards receiving last season. Now, he can suit up next to the best quarterback he's ever played with again. His 98.9 receiving yards per game when catching passes from Cousins are the most by a QB-WR duo all-time (min. 50 games).

Tuesday morning, it was reported that the Cincinnati Bengals were sending a sixth-round pick to the Philadelphia Eagles for pass rusher Arnold Ebiketie. This wouldn't have been a massive story, except the Bengals front office didn't realize they would also be giving up a third-round pick in the deal!

Nick Korte of Over The Cap pointed out that Ebiketie was signed as a compensatory free agent by the Eagles. The Bengals lost three compensatory free agents and signed two, with Cordell Volson not qualifying as a CFA lost since he hasn't played a snap this year. So, adding Ebiketie means the Bengals would lose a 2027 compensatory third-round pick that was coming after Trey Hendrickson signed with the Baltimore Ravens.

Oh that's brutal on the 3rd round comp pick! as noted by @nickkorte pic.twitter.com/OwwyNB71PA

From the NFL Collective Bargaining Agreement:

"If a Club trades for a player who was a CFA during that League Year, whether the player was another Club's CFA or the acquiring Club's own CFA, the player will be considered a CFA gained by the acquiring Club and will be included in the Compensatory netting process described above."

It's believed Cincinnati initially thought Volson counted toward the formula.

Naturally, when the Bengals discovered this, they backed out of the deal. This isn't even the first time Korte has had to correct the league about compensatory picks. He actually helped the Bengals secure a top-100 pick in the 2024 NFL Draft after a compensatory mix-up.

Duke Tobin and the Bengals look silly because they almost gave up a third-round pick for Ebiketie. They should hire Korte in the front office after helping them twice in three years.

There's no questioning the Jaguars' goal this season: They are all-in on a Super Bowl. Here's what they traded just this week:

Jacksonville saw Eric Murray go down with a season-ending injury, so they targeted Bates to replace him. He's tied for the second-most interceptions (28) since entering the league in 2018 and is the only NFL player to have recorded a pick-six in each of the last three seasons.

The Jaguars also lost defensive tackle DaVon Hamilton for the remainder of the season after he tore his quad, so the front office went out and acquired a couple of legitimate starters in Godchaux and Davis.

The Jaguars are "winners" because it's exciting when a team gets this aggressive and pushes all of their chips to the middle of the proverbial table. Fans have all the reason in the world to be excited about this run for the No. 1 seed in the AFC.

However, while Jacksonville's roster got better this week, I am questioning all the capital the Jags just spent.

Trading second- and fifth-round picks for Bates is expensive, but giving up first- and second-round picks for Davis was shocking. He's a solid player in the middle, but no one had any idea the Eagles would fetch that kind of return for him. He's recorded 31 tackles and 1.5 sacks in nine games played this season, and he recorded a career-high 4.5 sacks in 2025. Just eight months ago, Davis signed a three-year, $78 million extension that made him one of the highest-paid defensive tackles in the NFL.

If the Jaguars win the Super Bowl, then we can't complain about the compensation. But Jimmy James Gladstone is clearly in an "eff them picks" mood.

2027 second-round pick, 2028 fifth-round pick

2027 first-round pick, 2029 second-round pick

There was a lot of oxygen given to running backs at this year's trade deadline. The Colts' Jonathan Taylor was a player everyone was watching, the Ravens' Derrick Henry was another big name brought up in rumors, theTennessee Titans' Tony Pollard was viewed as a potential target and Breece Hall had seemingly fallen out of favor in New York with the emergence of Braelon Allen. However, not one running back was moved.

It's understandable if a team didn't want to trade a Christian McCaffrey-like package for Taylor, but what about Pollard? He shredded the Cleveland Browns for 129 yards and three touchdowns in Week 7, and then racked up 117 rushing yards and two touchdowns in Week 8 against the Bengals. He's averaging a career-high 74.4 rushing yards per game and ranks top 12 in rushing yards and rushing touchdowns this season. Pollard and Henry are the only players to have rushed for 1,000 yards in each of the last four seasons. Isn't that worth a fourth-round pick?

There were multiple Super Bowl or playoff hopefuls that could have used a new running back, such as the Vikings, Broncos, Green Bay Packers and New York Giants. Instead, Taylor, Henry, Pollard and Hall will continue running the ball for teams that won't be playing meaningful football in January.

The Chiefs have the No. 2 offense in the NFL through nine weeks, but this passing attack has struggled. They needed to make an addition at wide receiver at the trade deadline, and Brett Veach did just that by trading a fourth-round pick and a fifth-round pick to the Colts for Josh Downs and a seventh-rounder.

Downs has recorded the second-most receiving yards from the slot (1,995) since 2023, trailing only Amon-Ra St. Brown. While he's not exactly a "field stretcher" that is going to slice through defenses for 40-yard bombs, he still upgrades this passing offense.

Mahomes is actually the only quarterback in the past 20 years to average under 5.0 air yards per attempt in four straight games (min. 30 attempts).

The 5-2-1 Giants are the clear-cut frontrunners in one of the worst divisions in the NFL. However, John Harbaugh hasn't clinched the division just yet. The Giants did swing a trade for a new quarterback in J.J. McCarthy, but they were also interested in adding a star defensive tackle like Jeffery Simmons or a running back like Jonathan Taylor. Instead, they remained quiet on Tuesday.

We'll see if Cam Skattebo can carry this rushing attack for the remaining nine games and if the Giants can finish atop this weird NFC East. But Giants fans wanted to see Harbaugh make an addition on Tuesday.

The Eagles traded away two starters in the past few weeks, defensive tackle Jordan Davis and offensive lineman Cam Jurgens. Remember, they also traded star wideout A.J. Brown to the New England Patriots in June.

When you count up all the capital the Eagles acquired for those three players, Philly received two first-round picks, two second-round picks and two fifth-round picks. That's comparable to the Ricky Williams trade between Washington and New Orleans back in 1999.

Two 1st-round picks, two 3rd-round picks, 4th-round pick, 5th-round pick, 6th-round pick, 7th-round pick

Jordan Davis, Cam Jurgens, A.J. Brown haul

Two 1st-round picks, two 2nd-round picks, two 5th-round picks

One team that should have been in the running for Downs along with the Chiefs was the Los Angeles Rams. Davante Adams is on injured reserve with a hamstring injury, Puka Nacua is expected to join him with a knee issue, and Jordan Whittington was just placed on injured reserve due to a back injury. That means Tutu Atwell, Konata Mumpfield and CJ Daniels are Matthew Stafford's top receivers.

The 5-4 Rams are not the "Super Team" many thought they would be in 2026, and they didn't make any moves at the trade deadline in an attempt to right the ship. Sean McVay will try to grind out wins through Kyren Williams, Blake Corum and his tight ends.

The Jaguars orchestrated multiple trades to boost their roster with sights set on the No. 1 seed in the conference. How were the Buffalo Bills going to respond? On Tuesday, Brandon Beane sent a second-round pick to the Titans for safety Amani Hooker and a fifth-round pick.

Hooker has been a beast as of late. He powered the Titans to their first win of the season against the Colts with 13 tackles and an interception, then picked off Joe Burrow THREE times in Week 8. Hooker is just the third player in the past 40 years to begin a season with 40 tackles and five interceptions through seven games.

Following the release of C.J. Gardner-Johnson, the Bills needed to make an addition on the back end, and Hooker fits the bill. It was a pricey move, but I respect the swing from a Super Bowl-or-bust team.

Jaguars wide receiver Brian Thomas Jr. was viewed as a prime trade candidate this year, but he's staying put in Jacksonville. A change of scenery could have done him some good, but BTJ will just have to deal with being WR4 behind Parker Washington, Jakobi Meyers and Josh Cameron. His 117 yards receiving through nine weeks rank No. 7 on the team.

Remember, back in 2024, Thomas became the fourth rookie to record 1,200 yards receiving and catch double-digit touchdowns, joining Randy Moss, Odell Beckham Jr. and Ja'Marr Chase. However, he's quickly fallen out of favor in Jacksonville. Thomas has played just 43.1% of offensive snaps this season and has caught zero passes in three out of the last four games.

We saw two big-name defensive tackles traded this cycle in Jeffery Simmons and Jordan Davis. The Bears sent the Titans two first-round picks and a Day 3 pick swap for Simmons, while Davis went from Philly to Jacksonville for a first-round pick and a second-round pick.

Forget pass rushers, defensive tackles are the new premium position! There have been five first-round picks traded for veteran defensive tackles in the past two seasons. There have been just six first-round picks traded for all other veteran players in that same timeframe. Simmons, Davis, Dexter Lawrence and Quinnen Williams are the interior linemen who went for first-round picks in the last 365 days.

Palermo, Perin sicuro: "Meglio parare un rigore sotto la Nord che in Champions League"

Può un rigore parato sotto la Curva Nord del Barbera essere più significativo di uno parato in Champions League? A quanto pare sì. Il portiere del Palermo Mattia Perin infatti non ha avuto alcun dubbio a rispondere alla domanda di DAZN, nel corso di un’intervista realizzata al centro sportivo di Torretta, in cui gli si chiedeva di scegliere fra le due opzioni. “Parare un rigore sotto la Curva Nord” è stata la risposta secca del portiere rosanero

2026 NFL trade deadline tracker: Vikings reunite with Kirk Cousins as in-season moves wrap up

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

A steady stream of blockbuster moves in advance of the 2026 NFL trade deadline redistributed some of the league's top talent at numerous positions. A grand total of 18 in-season trades made the 2026 campaign the fourth-most active year of player movement and defined where teams stand in the playoff race and the hunt for the No. 1 overall pick in the 2027 draft.

Teams including the Chicago Bears and Jacksonville Jaguars went all-in ahead of the deadline when they spent premier draft capital on defensive standouts like Jeffery Simmons and Jordan Davis. Those swaps made the Super Bowl contenders two of the deadline's biggest winners.

On the other hand, the Indianapolis Colts emerged as by far the most aggressive sellers when they parted with Quenton Nelson, DeForest Buckner, Keenan Allen and Josh Downs. That dichotomy shows exactly how teams used Nov. 10 as a pivot point in their quest for either a championship or a jumpstart in the rebuilding process.

A bevy of big-name trade candidates stood still when the deadline passed. The Colts' fire sale did not extend to Jonathan Taylor or Daniel Jones, while defenders like Khalil Mack, Will McDonald and Budda Baker remained with their respective teams, too.

Below is every in-season trade processed in advance of the NFL's 2026 deadline.

Just over a month ago, the Ravens pulled off the league's third in-season trade to acquire Cam Jurgens. The move landed them a long-term solution at center following Tyler Linderbaum's exit in free agency. Now they have another center on the roster. Neuzil was the Falcons' starter at that position this season but appears poised to take on a backup role for the Ravens unless he moves to guard (a position he played to an extent in Atlanta and exclusively in college). Olaivavega Ioane is locked in at right guard, so it will come down to John Simpson and Neuzil on the left side. The other will head to the bench as the primary interior offensive line reserve.

The Falcons will not walk away empty-handed, as Vorhees brings starting experience at guard. There will be some shifting around to do on that offensive line.

Kansas City owns the 10th-best passing offense in the NFL but is more reliant on yards after the catch than any team in football. Additionally, wide receivers accounted for only 46.0% of the Chiefs' receiving yards through Week 9, the second-lowest rate, behind only the Raiders at 36.8%, according to Next Gen Stats. This team needed another weapon for Patrick Mahomes and secured one just minutes before the trade deadline in the form of Downs. Although he is not much of a field-stretcher, he could free up Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy or expand his own role now that he is on a new team.

That concludes the Colts' fire sale, bringing the tally to four players traded. All of them were on expiring contracts, so Indianapolis prioritized landing guaranteed draft capital for players likely to leave in free agency, who may or may not have netted compensatory picks. There are now only five healthy wide receivers on the Colts' active roster: Laquon Treadwell, Deion Burks, Ashton Dulin, Anthony Gould and Nick Westbrook-Ikhine.

One AFC West team got stronger while the other slipped further into the abyss. The Broncos added Daiyan Henley to their linebacker group and now own high-end depth behind locked-in starters Alex Singleton and Justin Strnad. Henley, a former third-round pick, was an every-week starter for the Chargers and brings excellent coverage and run defense skills to the vaunted Denver unit as it gears up for a postseason push. The Broncos anticipate receiving a compensatory pick for John Franklin-Myers' departure in free agency, which will help offset the cost of this deal.

It was puzzling to see the Chargers sit on their hands for most of the pre-deadline, considering they are the only winless team in the league, and while the Henley deal finally made them sellers, it was also a surprise for them to strengthen a division rival. They latched onto their other tradeable assets — players like Khalil Mack and even Justin Herbert, who would have commanded far greater returns— while securing only Day 3 capital for Henley.

For the second time in his career, Cousins is a Viking. Minnesota had the most glaring need at quarterback of any team in the league heading into deadline day, and Cousins was the most obvious target given his familiarity with the franchise and offensive system and his status as a bridge for the Raiders to No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza. He is coming off his worst performance of the year (a three-interception day in a loss to the 49ers) and seemed destined to cede the job to Mendoza in the weeks to come, so the trade makes all kinds of sense for both teams.

Minnesota's two losses this season emphasized its inability to capitalize on owning the league's stingiest defense. It was most apparent on "Monday Night Football" when Carson Wentz struggled mightily in a 23-6 loss to the Bills. Cousins' arrival gives the Vikings a rest-of-season solution and an upgrade over Wentz and Kyler Murray -- one who should be able to force-feed the ball to superstar wideout Justin Jefferson and more accurately distribute it to the secondary options, too.

The Vikings sent their own 2027 third-round pick to the Raiders in the deal and still possess the Eagles' third-rounder, which projects to be a more valuable asset. Las Vegas now owns more Day 2 capital and will put that to use in its construction of a young core around Mendoza.

Following the release of C.J. Gardner-Johnson, the Bills were down to the starting tandem of Cole Bishop and Damar Hamlin at safety. The depth in that group was minimal, and the starting production was that of the fourth-worst pass defense in the NFL. Enter Hooker, who is tied for the league lead with five interceptions. The Titans captain is in the midst of a career year and is allowing a passer rating of just 79.3, which is the best mark of his starting career and the best number since his rookie year when he played off the bench.

The rebuilding Titans now have two second-round picks to go with their two first-rounders in 2027. They are in the throes of a defensive tear-down after they also moved on from Jeffery Simmons and will be among the richest teams in draft capital as they work to stockpile young talent and building blocks for the long-term future.

San Francisco has been dealing with injuries in the receiver room all season. It started during training camp, when Ricky Pearsall went out for the year. It continued in Week 1 with De'Zhaun Stribling injuring his ankle. It got worse in recent weeks with both Mike Evans and Deebo Samuel Sr. experiencing injury issues of their own.

Now, the Niners are adding another veteran to the mix: six-time Pro-Bowler Keenan Allen. He's several years removed from playing at that Pro Bowler level, but he does have 40 catches for 351 yards and a touchdown with the Colts this season.

He should probably be expected to fill snaps at the Z receiver position and in the slot while Evans and Samuel are out. He'll likely rotate in with those guys, Stribling upon his return, and players like Demarcus Robinson and Jordan Watkins. His addition gives the 49ers a reliable option while they deal with injuries and provides solid depth when their main pieces return.

The Cincinnati Bengals were reportedly trading a sixth-round pick to the Eagles for pass rusher Arnold Ebiketie. However, the Bengals front office didn't realize they would also lose a third-round pick in the deal.

Nick Korte of Over The Cap pointed out that Ebiketie was signed by the Eagles as a compensatory free agent. The Bengals lost three compensatory free agents this offseason and signed two. With the addition of Ebiketie, the Bengals would lose a 2027 third-round compensatory pick that was coming after Trey Hendrickson signed with the Ravens.

About an hour later, the trade reportedly collapsed. Korte noted that the Bengals may have thought offensive lineman Cordell Volson counted as one of their CFAs lost. However, he has not played a single snap this year and does not count toward the formula.

Just when it seemed like the Jaguars had filled all of their needs, they went out and made their biggest move of all. Jacksonville's acquisition of Davis is a blockbuster in every sense of the word. The standout defensive tackle will be on the team's books through 2029 after signing a lucrative contract extension with the Eagles earlier this year and will be among the faces of the franchise as it pursues its first Super Bowl. His arrival marks the third trade for a defensive player in one day for Jacksonville and gives them a star in the trenches who should make an already superb group even more impenetrable.

On the other side, Philadelphia is officially in tear-down mode for 2026. Nothing signals a rebuild like moving a foundational player months after locking him into a new contract. The Eagles now have 10 picks in the first two rounds of the next three drafts and can seek an expedited return to playoff contention with a couple of massive draft day hauls.

The Lions should be better off than they were before in the defensive backfield with the addition of Cisco, but by how much? Cisco arrives in a unit that ranks dead last in stopping the pass at 260.9 yards per game. He slipped from his status as an every-week starter and was at the top of the Jets' depth chart for just four of nine games this season. While he remains productive, this is not a home-run transaction for a Detroit team that has to be better on his side of the ball in order to achieve its goals. This could age much better if and when Kerby Joseph returns to the lineup, because Cisco would be a pleasant depth option behind both Joseph and Brian Branch.

New York will have to wait a year to reap the benefits of this trade. It is a success to get something in return for a player who started fewer than half of his games this season, but the Jets need all the draft capital they can stockpile in order to pull themselves out of their rebuild, and 2028 is a long way away.

Jacksonville entered the week with needs at safety and defensive tackle and filled both of them in a matter of hours. Shortly after acquiring Jessie Bates III, the Jaguars made a play for Godchaux, boosting a defense that may already be Super Bowl-caliber. This is a necessary addition for general manager James Gladstone after he watched DaVon Hamilton go down with a torn quad in Week 8 and one that should keep the NFL's No. 5 run defense in the top echelon.

The pick heading to New Orleans will become a 2027 sixth-round pick if the Jaguars acquire one, according to ESPN. They do not currently own one but are projected to receive two compensatory sixth-round picks, according to Spotrac. Even with the caveat on the return, the Saints upgraded in the deal considering they spent a seventh-rounder to acquire Godchaux in a trade last year. He was a questionable fit in a run defense that ranks fifth-worst in the NFL, anyway.

The Jaguars secured a rental for their secondary and an impactful one at that. Bates is a perennial All-Pro safety who not only beefs up the starting lineup with renowned playmaking ability but also adds depth to a group that is dealing with injuries and just lost Eric Murray for the year with an elbow issue. Jacksonville is right in the midst of its contention window and is in play for the AFC's No. 1 seed, so any move that strengthens its Super Bowl chances is justifiable, even if it gives up quite a bit in the way of draft picks to pull it off.

Those picks make this deal a beneficial one for the Falcons, too. Atlanta should have expected to lose Bates in free agency this coming offseason, since he was not among the many players it extended this past summer. The second-round pick is better than any compensatory pick that would have come with his departure, and the 2028 fifth-rounder sweetened the deal even further. Now is the time for the Falcons to turn expiring contracts into future assets, because, at 3-6, they are unlikely to contend for anything beyond a potential first-place finish in the NFL's weakest division.

Baltimore agreed to trade for Cardinals QB Jacoby Brissett and completed the deal over the weekend after its "Thursday Night Football" loss. This was an expected move for the Ravens, because their quarterback situation went from bad to worse in Week 8. The highly anticipated return of starter Lamar Jackson lasted just one play. Jackson suffered a thumb injury and is now on injured reserve. Tyler Huntley, who started in place of Jackson, took over but also went down. He is out for the season with a turf toe injury. The team relied on emergency third quarterback Joe Fagnano to finish the game and started him on Thursday.

Brissett spent the last two seasons with the Cardinals, who benched him in his final game with the team and turned instead to Gardner Minshew. The return of draft capital for a quarterback who lost his job, was unhappy all offseason about his contract situation and projected as a bridge to an eventual first-round pick is a huge success for a rebuilding Arizona team.

In giving up two first-rounders to secure a perennial All-Pro defensive tackle, the Bears went all-in and opened their Super Bowl contention window. The transaction paid immediate dividends when Simmons logged a sack and led the team with six tackles in his Chicago debut: A commanding 34-17 road win over the Seahawks. Mortgaging so much of their future first-round capital showed that the Bears trust their roster, as currently constructed, to compete at the top of the league, and Simmons is the kind of game-changing talent worth the steep price. His production in the pass rush and in run defense could help Chicago maximize its high-octane unit on the other side of the ball led by Caleb Williams.

This move sends the Titans deeper into their rebuild. Questions lie ahead about whether to stick with Cam Ward at quarterback, and with the first-round picks they acquired from dealing Simmons, there will be curiosity as to whether they will use one to land a new signal-caller. Regardless of how they use the picks, moving on from one of the greatest players in franchise history shows how bleak the present is in Tennessee and that all eyes are on the future.

The seemingly nonstop onslaught of injuries hit the 49ers' defensive line, resulting in CJ West landing on injured reserve with a bone chip in his knee and Alfred Collins being ruled out for the year with a torn patellar tendon. That heightened the need for immediate help in the trenches, so the 49ers brought Buckner back after his six-plus years in Indianapolis. The 49ers selected Buckner in the first round of the 2016 draft, and the best years of his prime came during his time in San Francisco, so this is a welcome return for someone the franchise remembers fondly. Buckner remains one of the NFL's top pass-rushing interior defensive linemen and will aid in the 49ers' Super Bowl push.

Buckner's exit marked the second trade in one day for the slumping Colts, who are in the midst of tearing things down with multiple star players on the move and former general manager Chris Ballard out the door. There is an argument to be made that a Day 3 pick was an underwhelming return, but for a 32-year-old veteran on an expiring contract, the Colts were content to get at least something of value for a player who was likely to leave in free agency anyway. Buckner grew publicly frustrated with Indianapolis' struggles, which heightened the anticipation for his trade or an eventual departure at the end of his contract.

Seahawks general manager Jon Schneider is keen to make big-time moves ahead of the trade deadline. This is the fourth consecutive year in which Seattle made a play for an impactful midseason addition to its roster. Nelson is as stellar of an offensive lineman as there is on the trade market this year as a six-time All-Pro and should elevate a struggling Seahawks run game over the long term, even if his arrival did not deliver an immediate boost in the loss to the Bears in his debut. This is also a savvy transaction for the front office given that it stands to receive at least one compensatory third-round pick in 2027; trading a third-rounder for Nelson was thus an easy price to pay.

The Colts got more in return for Nelson than they did for Buckner, which is to be expected considering his status as perhaps the top interior lineman in football. This is still a discount, though, and they remain without a first-round pick in either of the next two drafts. If they can use the third-rounder and next year's fifth-rounder to move up in the order, the trade deadline sale could prove more worthwhile.

Harrison reunited with his college quarterback, C.J. Stroud, upon his arrival in Houston. Will that help him rekindle what has become a disappointing career? His first two games with the Texans were mostly underwhelming, but he did haul in a touchdown reception in Week 7 and could secure a more sizable target share as the team seeks for a go-to No. 2 option behind star pass-catcher Nico Collins. This is trending toward a lost season for the Texans, but they will have another year of control over Harrison and the option to pick up a fifth year in 2028. If they can mold him into the star he was supposed to become out of Ohio State, their passing game will be in excellent shape over the long haul.

Trading Harrison was an admission that he was a bust of a former first-round pick, but to get a second-rounder in the process and to upgrade with a Day 3 pick swap was about as good of a deal as the Cardinals could have landed. Harrison ran his course in the desert and was clearly a poor fit in Mike LaFleur's system, so maximizing his value in the trade was a big win for a team that is not going to contend for anything meaningful in 2026 anyway.

The interior offensive line in Baltimore was a glaring issue last year and into the start of 2026. The Jurgens trade addressed that head-on. Not only did the Ravens find their long-term replacement for Tyler Linderbaum, who left in free agency, but the addition also allowed them to move Olaivavega Ioane from center back to right guard. The full benefits of this move may not show up until Lamar Jackson returns to action, whether that be later in 2026 or next season.

On the Eagles' side, Jurgens' exit effectively marked the end of an era for a once-legendary offensive line that also saw longtime tackle Lane Johnson and legendary position coach Jeff Stoutland enter retirement. It is a turning of the page and one that netted a couple of draft picks for a franchise that now looks destined to rebuild following a six-game losing streak. Philadelphia had been playing Jurgens out of position, anyway.

If Porter cannot fix the Cowboys' defense, then that unit might be broken beyond repair in 2026. Dallas made a splash to address its greatest weakness and has thus far seen the exact kind of individual impact it envisioned from the star cornerback, as he allowed a diminutive 39.6 passer rating and just three completions on 11 targets across his first four games with the team. Still, the unit around him is in shambles. And with Porter set to reach unrestricted free agency this offseason, this could be a one-year rental that fails to produce a postseason appearance.

The Steelers made it clear that they were not going to shell out market-resetting money to keep Porter around on a long-term contract extension. By declining to meet his demands as he sat out the start of the season, they only had one viable option, which was to trade him. A first-round pick would have been the preferable return package, but Pittsburgh received the next-best thing with both a second-rounder and 2027 draft capital. That sixth-round pick in next year's draft already vested, as it was conditional upon Porter being active for just two games with the Cowboys.

The torn knee ligaments Jaxson Dart sustained in Week 2 made the Giants desperate for quarterback help, because the rest of the roster remained talented enough to compete for the NFC East crown. The McCarthy acquisition at first came across as puzzling given his own struggles to stay healthy, his underwhelming play when available and the fact that New York already has a franchise quarterback and thus has little to gain from McCarthy potentially developing into a viable starter. But it has aged better than expected. McCarthy threw for 243 yards and a touchdown in the fourth quarter in Week 6, earned the starting job over Jameis Winston and promptly delivered a road win over the Texans to place the Giants in command of the division.

Getting anything in return for McCarthy after a disastrous start to his career was a win for the Vikings. He slipped all the way down to the third line of Minnesota's depth chart behind a couple of low-ceiling veterans in Kyler Murray and Carson Wentz and was clearly no longer part of the franchise's plans.

Cardinals place QB Gardner Minshew and WR Michael Wilson on IR

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Cardinals placed quarterback Gardner Minshew and wide receiver Michael Wilson on injured reserve Tuesday, the team announced.

Minshew tore the UCL in his elbow and will miss the rest of the season. Wilson’s neck injury will end his season.

“Not the ending I had envisioned, but grateful for the journey,” Wilson wrote on social media. “Regardless of what happens, my love for the game will never change! Thank you for all the love and support. The story is still being written.”

Wilson played eight games in 2026, making 51 receptions for 546 yards and two touchdowns.

In two appearances, Minshew completed 38 of 53 passes for 354 yards and three touchdowns, and led the Cardinals to an overtime win in Dallas in Week 8. He is scheduled to be a free agent at the end of the season.

The Cardinals signed veteran quarterback Josh Johnson and rookie quarterback Joey Aguilar to back up rookie Carson Beck. Beck will get his first NFL start on Sunday against the Rams.

Aguilar was on the Jaguars’ practice squad, and Johnson was on the Bengals’ practice squad.

The Cardinals also signed receiver Phillip Dorsett II to the practice squad and released tight end Rivaldo Fairweather and running back Jermar Jefferson from the practice squad.

Knack: Cloud Engineer

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Location: US Based (Full-Time, Remote) or International Contractor (Full-Time, Remote)

If US-Based:This will be a remote position based in the United States. Knack offers a complete benefits package to those working in the United States. Employees must be available during standard US business hours and maintain their primary work location within the US. Any extended time working outside the United States requires prior written approval.

International Contractor:This is a full-time, remote position, but candidates must have four hours overlap with Eastern timezone.

Knack runs thousands of customer applications across multiple global regions, including dedicated private environments. As a Cloud Engineer, you’ll own and evolve that infrastructure, harden it to meet SOC 2, HIPAA, and customer security requirements, and build the infrastructure behind our growing AI capabilities.

We’re an AI-forward team. You don’t need to type every Terraform block, but you do need to understand what’s happening, direct AI tools well, verify their work, and own the outcome. This role includes a weekly 24/7 on-call rotation.

Knack is an AI-powered no-code software development platform enabling organizations to manage, analyze, and share business data. The no-code market is growing at over 30% annually and projected to be almost $200B by 2030. Founded in 2010, Knack has grown into a leading player in the no-code movement, enabling businesses, teams, and individuals to build custom applications without writing a single line of code.

Serving a wide range of industries, from healthcare and education to finance and nonprofits, and organizations from small businesses to Fortune 500s, Knack enables users to boost data visibility and streamline operations.

To date, the platform has powered 400,000 apps and stored more than 5 billion data records for over 15,000 customers. Its enterprise-grade functionality includes an AI-powered visual builder, robust data structures, user access controls, and automation capabilities, all delivered by a fully remote team committed to trust, continuous improvement, and measurable impact.

Run AWS infrastructure as code, with AI in the loop

Implement security controls with our security lead

Lead OS upgrades, runtime and end-of-life migrations, dependency updates, and certificate renewals.

Operate and scale databases and customer environments

Resolve escalations and automate repeat work

Participate in our weekly 24/7 on-call rotation, responding to production alerts and following up with fixes or runbook updates so the same issue does not repeat.

Daily, practical use of AI tools like Claude, with the judgment to critically review AI-generated scripts, Terraform, and IAM policies, and accountability for everything you ship.

Interviews will be recorded and transcribed by an AI notetaking tool so our team can focus on the conversation and review it accurately later. Notes are used only for evaluating your application. You can opt out at any time by letting your recruiter know.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.
